# Suggested status changes (TPTP v10.0.0 -> verified result)

42 problems whose TPTP status appears to be **wrong**, 49 problems whose formulation appears to be buggy (missing axioms or hypotheses), and 507 problems with status Unknown/Open that are now settled. Of these, 7 wrong statuses and 197 settled problems concern problems that include NUM004-0; they are marked in the tables, and their status under a corrected NUM004-0 is in the section [Fixing NUM004-0](#fixing-num004-0). Likewise, 12 wrong statuses and 51 settled problems include the inconsistent curve axioms GEO004±0 ([Fixing GEO004](#fixing-geo004)). In all, 97 of the 507 settled problems are settled only through an inconsistent axiom set: their proof refutes the axioms alone and does not use the conjecture; they are two findings (NUM004-0 and GEO004±0 are inconsistent) rather than 97 separate results.

> ⚠️ **Warning: the axiom set SET004-0 + SET004-1 + NUM004-0 (Quaife's NBG with ordinals) is inconsistent**, so every one of the 277 TPTP problems that include NUM004-0 is Unsatisfiable. They appear in the tables below with a note; the cause, a fix, and the status of these problems with the fix are in the section [Fixing NUM004-0](#fixing-num004-0), near the end.

> ⚠️ **Warning: the curve axioms GEO004+0 and GEO004-0 (Kulik and Eschenbach) are inconsistent**, so every one of the 154 TPTP problems that include them is Unsatisfiable (a Theorem). They appear in the tables below with a note; the cause, a fix (consistent, with a Lean model), and the status of these problems with the fix are in the last section, [Fixing GEO004](#fixing-geo004).

| 🔴 | [**NUM333+1**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/NUM/NUM333%2B1.p) | ~~Theorem~~ | **CounterSatisfiable** | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite, countable: an inductive type)](Solutions/NUM/NUM333+1/ClaudeOpus---5.5.CSA-Mod.s): RDN arithmetic: associativity of sum for ALL elements. The sum axioms only produce sums of numerals and require sum to be functional in each pair of arguments, so on non-numerals it is unconstrained: with fresh elements j0..j6, sum(j0,j1,j3), sum(j3,j2,j4), sum(j1,j2,j5), sum(j0,j5,j6) violate associativity (j4 != j6). Lean-checked countermodel. (Associativity restricted to numerals is presumably the intended theorem.) |

| 🔴 | [**SET502-6**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET502-6.p) | ~~Satisfiable~~ | **Unsatisfiable** | [✅ GDV-checked proof (58 steps)](Solutions/SET/SET502-6/ClaudeOpus---5.5.UNS-CRf.s): Quaife NBG (SET004-0/-1). Our proof is GDV-verified (Vampire) and checked step by step with E; it uses only the problem's own axioms, through a small library of membership lemmas (domain, inverse, range, image, union, compose, identity). So the clauses are unsatisfiable, and the status Satisfiable is wrong. Statement: diagonalise(compose(r,inverse(s))) = complement(range_of(intersection(r,s))). |
| 🔴 | [**SEU260+1**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SEU/SEU260%2B1.p) | ~~CounterSatisfiable~~ | **Theorem** | [✅ GDV-checked proof (20 steps)](Solutions/SEU/SEU260+1/ClaudeOpus---5.5.THM-Ref.s): MPTP t53_wellord1 (bushy). Our proof is GDV-verified (Vampire) and checked step by step with E, so the conjecture follows from the axioms: the status is Theorem, and CounterSatisfiable is wrong. |

Our proof is GDV-verified (Vampire) and checked step by step with E, so the conjecture follows from the axioms: the status is Theorem, and CounterSatisfiable is wrong. |
| 🔴 | [**ALG301-1**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/ALG/ALG301-1.p) | ~~Satisfiable~~ | **Open** | no solution file: The single identity is the Equational Theories Project law E5093, which has no nontrivial finite model (Kisielewicz 1997, who mentioned it only as a possible Austin law). Whether it has a nontrivial (necessarily infinite) model is open: the ETP blueprint (chapter 'Order 5 Austin laws') lists E5093 and its dual E41179 in Table 20.2, 'trivial finite models, unknown infinite models', and neither is among the 32 order-5 Austin laws certified in arXiv:2609.05690 (Sept 2026). Its sibling ALG300-1 is Kisielewicz's proven Austin law E28770 (Table 20.1). So the status Satisfiable is unsupported; if ALG301-1 was meant to be another known Austin law, the formula was mis-transcribed. This entry rests on the literature only; it is not mechanically checked. |

## 🟠 Problem formulation appears to be buggy (49 problems)

**Every entry in this table has a verified model**: its solution file contains a Lean model of the problem exactly as written in TPTP v10.0.0 (axioms plus negated conjecture), checked with `Tools/check_lean_model.sh`. So the status of each problem as written is settled: it is not a theorem. The reason, however, is the formulation: a symbol in the conjecture has no (or too few) axioms, or a hypothesis is missing. The suggested change is therefore to **fix the problem**, since as written it does not express the intended theorem (details in the % Status line of each solution file). **The suggested fixes are our reading of the intended problem and have not been checked**: only the model of the problem as written is verified. (A fix should come with a patched file, a proof of the conjecture, and a model of the patched axioms, so that it cannot work by making the axioms inconsistent; this is not done yet.)

| | Problem | TPTP v10.0.0 | Verified model (problem as written) | Cause and suggested fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🟠 | [**FLD095-2**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/FLD/FLD095-2.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: ℚ): Satisfiable](Solutions/FLD/FLD095-2/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | with u=a/b, v=c/d, w=(a+c)/(b+d), the hypotheses only give b<0<d, and u<=v does not imply u<=w when b+d<0 (a=0, b=-2, c=d=1: u=0, v=1, w=-1). **Suggested fix (not checked):** add the missing sign hypothesis on b+d (or on b), as in the intended 'difficult inequality'. |
| 🟠 | [**GEO004-2**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/GEO/GEO004-2.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: ℝ²): Satisfiable](Solutions/GEO/GEO004-2/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the intended theorem is that a point with the property exists, but the conjecture is stated for the term midpoint(a,b), and midpoint is a function symbol that no axiom mentions. So a model may interpret it as any point: in the Lean model (the real plane with Tarski's betweenness and congruence, lean/Tarski in the project; a = (0,0), b = (1,0)) midpoint(a,b) := a, which fails the property. **Suggested fix (not checked):** use a variable instead of the term (~ between(a,M,b) | ~ equidistant(...) for all M), or include the axioms that define midpoint. |
| 🟠 | [**GEO005-2**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/GEO/GEO005-2.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: ℝ²): Satisfiable](Solutions/GEO/GEO005-2/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the intended theorem is that a point with the property exists, but the conjecture is stated for the term apex(a,b), and apex is a function symbol that no axiom mentions. So a model may interpret it as any point: in the Lean model (the real plane with Tarski's betweenness and congruence, lean/Tarski in the project; a = (0,0), b = (1,0)) apex(a,b) := a, which fails the property. **Suggested fix (not checked):** use a variable instead of the term (~ between(a,M,b) | ~ equidistant(...) for all M), or include the axioms that define apex. |
| 🟠 | [**GEO071-2**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/GEO/GEO071-2.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: ℝ²): Satisfiable](Solutions/GEO/GEO071-2/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the hypotheses (uv ≡ wx, vw ≡ xu, uw ≡ vx, y between u and w and between v and x, u ≠ v, x ≠ u) hold in every rectangle u,v,w,x with centre y, where u = w fails. Lean model: the real plane with Tarski's betweenness and congruence (lean/Tarski in the project), u,v,w,x the unit square, y its centre. So the conjecture is not a consequence of the axioms as stated. **Suggested fix (not checked):** add the missing hypothesis of the 'non-collinear points theorem' it is a corollary of (presumably that u, v, w are collinear). |

| 🟠 | [**SET445-6**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET445-6.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet): Satisfiable](Solutions/SET/SET445-6/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the intended theorem is that adding a pair (y,z) with y not in the domain of a function x gives a function, but y and z are not required to be sets. With z the universal class, ordered_pair(null_class, universal_class) is the set {{null_class}}, which is not an ordered pair, so x union {it} is not a subclass of the cross product and not a function (Lean model: x = null_class, y = null_class, z = universal_class). **Suggested fix (not checked):** add member(y,universal_class) and member(z,universal_class) as hypotheses. |

**Not in this table: 28 further formulation bugs in the GEO004 problems**, which only show once the inconsistent GEO004 axioms are fixed (with the published axioms every conjecture follows, so no model of the problem as written can exist). 26 problems (GEO130–136, GEO140–145, ±1) use a predicate `between/4` that no axiom defines (`between_o` is meant; each patched problem is proved), and GEO151±1 (T17) has X where the other object Y is meant. Each has a Lean countermodel with the fixed axioms; see [Fixing GEO004](#fixing-geo004).

| [GEO007-2](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/GEO/GEO007-2.p) | Unknown | Unsatisfiable | [✅ GDV-checked proof (226 steps)](Solutions/GEO/GEO007-2/ClaudeOpus---5.5.UNS-Ref.s). Tarski geometry in Quaife's clause form (GEO002-0), bare axioms. The argument follows Schwabhäuser, Szmielew and Tarski (Metamathematische Methoden in der Geometrie, 1983), chapters 2-5; the proof plans of the longer lemmas (SST 4.16-4.19 and Gupta's theorem 5.1) were taken from Beeson and Wos's OTTER proofs (Finding proofs in Tarskian geometry, J. Autom. Reasoning 2017), translated into Quaife's notation, with every step re-proved by E from its listed parents. The concepts Col, E3, IFS and FS appear as definition steps. Statement: two points on the ray from a away from c are ordered: SST 5.2 applied to the reversed segments. |
| [GEO008-2](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/GEO/GEO008-2.p) | Unknown | Unsatisfiable | [✅ GDV-checked proof (244 steps)](Solutions/GEO/GEO008-2/ClaudeOpus---5.5.UNS-Ref.s). Tarski geometry in Quaife's clause form (GEO002-0), bare axioms. The argument follows Schwabhäuser, Szmielew and Tarski (Metamathematische Methoden in der Geometrie, 1983), chapters 2-5; the proof plans of the longer lemmas (SST 4.16-4.19 and Gupta's theorem 5.1) were taken from Beeson and Wos's OTTER proofs (Finding proofs in Tarskian geometry, J. Autom. Reasoning 2017), translated into Quaife's notation, with every step re-proved by E from its listed parents. The concepts Col, E3, IFS and FS appear as definition steps. Statement: B(a,c,e), B(a,d,e), B(c,b,d) imply B(a,b,e): SST 5.3 gives the order of c and d, then transitivity of betweenness (SST 3.5-3.7). |
| [GEO009-2](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/GEO/GEO009-2.p) | Unknown | Unsatisfiable | [✅ GDV-checked proof (239 steps)](Solutions/GEO/GEO009-2/ClaudeOpus---5.5.UNS-Ref.s). Tarski geometry in Quaife's clause form (GEO002-0), bare axioms. The argument follows Schwabhäuser, Szmielew and Tarski (Metamathematische Methoden in der Geometrie, 1983), chapters 2-5; the proof plans of the longer lemmas (SST 4.16-4.19 and Gupta's theorem 5.1) were taken from Beeson and Wos's OTTER proofs (Finding proofs in Tarskian geometry, J. Autom. Reasoning 2017), translated into Quaife's notation, with every step re-proved by E from its listed parents. The concepts Col, E3, IFS and FS appear as definition steps. Statement: SST Satz 5.3 (inner connectivity): B(a,c,e), B(a,d,e) imply B(a,c,d) or B(a,d,c). |
| [GEO012-2](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/GEO/GEO012-2.p) | Unknown | Unsatisfiable | [✅ GDV-checked proof (256 steps)](Solutions/GEO/GEO012-2/ClaudeOpus---5.5.UNS-Ref.s). Tarski geometry in Quaife's clause form (GEO002-0, GEO002-1), bare axioms. The argument follows Schwabhäuser, Szmielew and Tarski (Metamathematische Methoden in der Geometrie, 1983), chapters 2-5; the proof plans of the longer lemmas (SST 4.16-4.19 and Gupta's theorem 5.1) were taken from Beeson and Wos's OTTER proofs (Finding proofs in Tarskian geometry, J. Autom. Reasoning 2017), translated into Quaife's notation, with every step re-proved by E from its listed parents. The concepts Col, E3, IFS and FS appear as definition steps. Statement: a ≠ b, Col(a,b,c), Col(a,b,d) imply Col(a,c,d) and Col(b,c,d). Lemma: a ≠ b, Col(a,b,c), Col(a,b,d) ⊢ Col(a,c,d), by the nine cases of colinearity4 and SST 5.1-5.3. |

| [NUM163-1](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/NUM/NUM163-1.p) | Unknown | Unsatisfiable | [✅ GDV-checked proof (78 steps)](Solutions/NUM/NUM163-1/ClaudeOpus---5.5.UNS-CRf.s) (via the inconsistency of SET004-0 + SET004-1 + NUM004-0, see [Fixing NUM004-0](#fixing-num004-0)). **Note:** this status rests only on the inconsistency. The intended theorem is "the union of two inductive classes is inductive", but the second hypothesis reads K(x) ⊆ y (with K(z) = {∅} ∪ image(successor_relation, z)) where K(y) ⊆ y is surely meant. As written the problem is false in standard NBG: take x = On and y = On ∪ {a} for a set a that is not an ordinal; then K(x) ⊆ x and K(x) ⊆ y, but succ(a) is not in x ∪ y. With K(y) ⊆ y the theorem holds, since K(x ∪ y) = K(x) ∪ K(y). Suggested fix: replace image(successor_relation,x) by image(successor_relation,y) in prove_inductive_closed_under_union_2. With the fixed NUM004-0 a Lean model (x = ω, y = ω ∪ {ω}) shows that the conjecture as written does not follow, and the patched problem is proved (GDV-checked, Fixed/NUM004-0/patched/NUM163-1.UNS-CRf.s).. **Note:** this status rests only on the inconsistency: with the fixed NUM004-0 the conjecture is not a consequence of the axioms (Lean model: [Fixed/NUM004-0/NUM163-1.SAT-Mod.s](Fixed/NUM004-0/NUM163-1.SAT-Mod.s)) |

## Fixing NUM004-0

### The inconsistency

Clause `rest_of4` of NUM004-0 puts (u, restrict(x,u,universal_class)) into rest_of(x) for every u in domain_of(x), even when restrict(x,u,universal_class) is a proper class, while `rest_of1` makes rest_of(x) a subclass of cross_product(universal_class,universal_class), whose pairs have set components (`cartesian_product2`). With x = cross_product(universal_class,universal_class) and u = {null_class}, u x V would be a set, hence so would its range, the universal class; then diagonalise(element_relation) would be a set (Russell's paradox). The 78-step refutation uses only the axioms and is GDV-verified ([NUM289-1](Solutions/NUM/NUM289-1/), the axioms alone). Consequences for the 277 TPTP v10.0.0 problems that include NUM004-0: the 7 with status Satisfiable are wrong (all listed below), the 197 with status Unknown are Unsatisfiable (all listed below; 153 of them also with a direct proof that does not rely on the inconsistency), and the 73 Unsatisfiable ones are trivially so. SUGGESTED FIX: add the literal `~ member(V,universal_class)` to `rest_of4` (the restriction must be a set, as the class existence theorem in the clause's comment requires); requiring function(X) would also do. **With this fix the axioms are consistent**: a Lean model of SET004-0 + SET004-1 + the fixed NUM004-0 is in [Fixed/NUM004-0](Fixed/NUM004-0/) (check with `Fixed/NUM004-0/check.sh NUM289-1`). Of the proofs here, the 153 direct proofs of Unknown problems and the proofs of NUM036-1, NUM097-1, NUM109-1, NUM173-1, NUM175-1 and NUM252-1 do not use `rest_of4`, so they remain valid with the fix. Of the 73 Unsatisfiable problems, 63 have a TSTP derivation that does not use `rest_of4`. One, [NUM219-1](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/NUM/NUM219-1.p) (domain_of(rest_of(u)) = domain_of(u), rating 1.00), is false with the fix (Lean countermodel in the same folder, u = V × V), so it holds only through the inconsistency.

### Status of the 277 problems with the axioms as published and with the fix

With the axioms as published every problem is Unsatisfiable (the axioms alone are contradictory). The columns after the TPTP status give the verifiable status with the fixed `rest_of4` and its evidence: a proof that does not cite `rest_of4` is valid with the fixed axioms unchanged (checked by its leaves, and re-checked with GDV against the fixed axiom file), and a Lean model in [Fixed/NUM004-0](Fixed/NUM004-0/) shows that the conjecture does not follow. With the fix: **230 Unsatisfiable, 38 Satisfiable, 9 Unknown** (Unknown means that the only proofs found so far use `rest_of4`; work on these continues). **How to read the count of our proofs that hold with the fix:** for 119 problems our proof was found before or independently of the inconsistency and never used `rest_of4`. For the 26 problems marked "found in this work on the fix" (and NUM046-1, NUM065-1, NUM149-1 and NUM201-1, which had no proof of ours before), the only proof we had was the refutation of the axioms; after the inconsistency was found they were re-proved without `rest_of4`, and the new direct proof replaced the refutation in this repository (the refutation is no longer published). So for these, "same file as published" is the result of that re-proving. 🔴 marks a TPTP v10.0.0 status that is wrong for the axioms as published (Satisfiable, although the axioms are contradictory). The status after the fix is new information about a corrected axiom set, not a correction of TPTP v10.0.0.

|  | [NUM163-1](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/NUM/NUM163-1.p) | Unknown | **Satisfiable** | [✅ Lean-checked model](Fixed/NUM004-0/NUM163-1.SAT-Mod.s) (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet). **Note:** this status rests only on the inconsistency. The intended theorem is "the union of two inductive classes is inductive", but the second hypothesis reads K(x) ⊆ y (with K(z) = {∅} ∪ image(successor_relation, z)) where K(y) ⊆ y is surely meant. As written the problem is false in standard NBG: take x = On and y = On ∪ {a} for a set a that is not an ordinal; then K(x) ⊆ x and K(x) ⊆ y, but succ(a) is not in x ∪ y. With K(y) ⊆ y the theorem holds, since K(x ∪ y) = K(x) ∪ K(y). Suggested fix: replace image(successor_relation,x) by image(successor_relation,y) in prove_inductive_closed_under_union_2. With the fixed NUM004-0 a Lean model (x = ω, y = ω ∪ {ω}) shows that the conjecture as written does not follow, and the patched problem is proved (GDV-checked, Fixed/NUM004-0/patched/NUM163-1.UNS-CRf.s). |

## Fixing GEO004

### The inconsistency

GEO004+0 and its clausification GEO004-0 encode the simple curve geometry of L. Kulik and C. Eschenbach, *A Geometry of Oriented Curves* (report, University of Hamburg, 1999). There the sum c1 ⊕ c2 is a **partial** operation (Def. 2.3: "The operation sum is only partially defined: Two curves need not have a curve as their sum since curves are connected and do not branch"; axiom C8 asserts it only for curves that meet). In TPTP, `sum` is a **total** function symbol: `sum_defn` with C := sum(C1,C2) says that the union of *any* two curves is a curve. That makes the axioms inconsistent.

**Argument** (only the definitions and C3, C4 are used). Let C = C1 ∪ C2 with meet(P,C1,C2), and let E be on C, say on C1. Split C1 at an inner point (C3, C4) into D1 ∪ D2, with E on D1, and take inner points T of D2 and S of C2. Then C1 and sum(D1,C2) are parts of C through E, and neither is part of the other: T is on C1 but not on sum(D1,C2), and S is on sum(D1,C2) but not on C1. So E is not an endpoint of C. Every curve splits like this (C3, C4), so **no curve has an endpoint**. But meet(P,C1,C2) from C4 makes P an endpoint of C1. (The step that fails in the paper is the curve sum(D1,C2): D1 and C2 do not meet.)

The checked refutation has 36 steps and cites only axioms, so the same file refutes every problem; it is our solution of each of the 154 problems (e.g. [GEO114-1](Solutions/GEO/GEO114-1/), cnf, and [GEO115+1](Solutions/GEO/GEO115+1/), fof; GDV-verified for each). Consequences for the 154 TPTP v10.0.0 problems that include GEO004+0 or GEO004-0: the 12 with status Satisfiable or CounterSatisfiable are wrong (all listed below), the 51 with status Unknown are Unsatisfiable/Theorem, but only through the inconsistency, and the 91 with status Unsatisfiable/Theorem are trivially so.

### The fix

SUGGESTED FIX, following the paper: guard the definition of the sum by the condition of C8,

    fof(sum_defn,axiom, ! [C,C1,C2] : ( ? [P] : meet(P,C1,C2) => ( C = sum(C1,C2) <=> ! [Q] : ( incident_c(Q,C) <=> ( incident_c(Q,C1) | incident_c(Q,C2) ) ) ) ) ).

(in GEO004-0: the literal `~ meet(SMP,C1,C2)` added to each of the clauses sum_defn_4 to sum_defn_12). The function symbol stays, so every problem file is unchanged; sum(C1,C2) is unconstrained for curves that do not meet. **With this fix the axioms are consistent**: a Lean model of the fixed GEO004+0 together with GEO004+1, +2 and +3 (every problem includes a subset of these) is in [Fixed/GEO004](Fixed/GEO004/) (check with `Fixed/GEO004/check.sh`). The model: domain ℚ, where each element is at the same time a rational point, a closed rational interval and an oriented interval (via bijections of ℚ with the intervals and with the intervals × {up, down}); sum is the convex hull, which is the union when two intervals meet; every object moves through [0,1] in step. The same model is rejected against the unfixed axioms, as it must be.

### A formulation bug hidden by the inconsistency

26 problems (GEO130–136 and GEO140–145, fof and cnf: Theorems 4.15–4.19, 4.23, 4.24 and Corollaries 4.16, 4.18 of the report) state their conjecture with a predicate `between(O,P,Q,R)` that no axiom file defines; the axioms define `between_o`, the report's betweenness on oriented curves. With the published axioms this cannot be noticed, since everything follows from the inconsistency. With the fix, each of the 26 has a Lean countermodel (🟠 in the table), and each patched version (`between` → `between_o`) is proved, so the suggested fix is checked. A second, smaller slip: GEO151±1 (Theorem T17 of Eschenbach, Habel and Kulik) has X where the English text means the other object Y (`at(X,Q2)`, `trajectory_of(X)`); as written it has a Lean countermodel with the fix.

### Status of the 154 problems with the axioms as published and with the fix

With the axioms as published every problem is Unsatisfiable. The columns after the TPTP status give the verifiable status with the fixed `sum_defn` and its evidence. Only evidence that is valid with the fix is listed: a TSTP derivation that does not use `sum_defn` (it never mentions sum, or all its leaves are named and none is sum_defn) is a derivation from the fixed axioms as well, since every other formula is unchanged. Our own 36-step refutation uses `sum_defn` and does not count here. With the fix: **118 Unsatisfiable/Theorem, 36 Satisfiable/CounterSatisfiable, 0 Unknown** (Satisfiable: a Lean model of the problem with the fixed axioms in [Fixed/GEO004](Fixed/GEO004/); for the cnf versions the model also interprets the Skolem functions of the clausification. Unknown: no derivation without `sum_defn` and no model is known yet; work on these continues). 🔴 marks a TPTP v10.0.0 status that is wrong for the axioms as published (Satisfiable or CounterSatisfiable, although the axioms are contradictory). The status after the fix is new information about a corrected axiom set, not a correction of TPTP v10.0.0.

|  | [GEO151+1](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/GEO/GEO151%2B1.p) | Unknown | **CounterSatisfiable** | 🟠 [✅ Lean-checked model](Fixed/GEO004/GEO151+1.SAT-Mod.s) (infinite: ℚ intervals; events are intervals of instants): the conjecture does not follow. The English text says "If an object is in a position before and after another object moves, then it stays in this position while the other one moves", but the formula ends in `at(X,Q2)` and `between_o(trajectory_of(X),...)`, where the other object Y is meant; as written it asks X to be at P and at Q2 at the same time. The axioms do not make `at_the_same_time` transitive, and in the model X waits at P while Y passes Q1 and Q3. **Suggested fix:** `at(Y,Q2)` and `trajectory_of(Y)`; the patched problem is a theorem: [✅ GDV-checked proof](Fixed/GEO004/patched/GEO151+1.THM-Ref.s) ([patched file](Fixed/GEO004/patched/GEO151+1.p)) |
|  | [GEO151-1](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/GEO/GEO151-1.p) | Unknown | **Satisfiable** | 🟠 [✅ Lean-checked model](Fixed/GEO004/GEO151-1.SAT-Mod.s) (infinite: ℚ intervals; events are intervals of instants): the conjecture does not follow. The English text says "If an object is in a position before and after another object moves, then it stays in this position while the other one moves", but the formula ends in `at(X,Q2)` and `between_o(trajectory_of(X),...)`, where the other object Y is meant; as written it asks X to be at P and at Q2 at the same time. The axioms do not make `at_the_same_time` transitive, and in the model X waits at P while Y passes Q1 and Q3. **Suggested fix:** `at(Y,Q2)` and `trajectory_of(Y)`; the patched problem is a theorem: [✅ GDV-checked proof](Fixed/GEO004/patched/GEO151-1.UNS-CRf.s) ([patched file](Fixed/GEO004/patched/GEO151-1.p)) |
